\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2\Delta t}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big)-\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1})\big)\Big]+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2\tau_{1}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\alpha\kappa\delta^{2}}{2}\left\lvert{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\rvert^{2}-\kappa\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\leq 0.\end{split}

(4.31)

We deduce from (
4.30
) and (
4.31
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{B}{2\Delta t}\Big(\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)+\int_{\Omega}\frac{A}{\Delta t}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\psi(\varphi_{h}^{n})-\psi(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{2\Delta t}\Big(\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}-\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{2\Delta t}\Big(\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa}{2\Delta t}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big)-\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1})\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+m_{0}\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+n_{0}\left\|{\chi_{\sigma}\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+K\chi_{\sigma}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}+2\eta_{0}\|{\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{\alpha\kappa\delta^{2}}{2}\left\|{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2\tau_{1}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{n}\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}+\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)-\mu_{h}^{n}\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}+\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big)-\frac{\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[K\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})-K\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}+\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)\Big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\\
&\leq 0.\end{split}

(4.32)

For the terms in (
4.32
) involving the boundary integrals, we have by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, (
4.9
), (
4.10
) and the trace theorem, that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[K\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})-K\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}+\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)\Big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\\
&\leq KC_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}\left(\frac{\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2\chi_{\sigma}}+1\right)\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{3\chi_{\sigma}K}{4}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}+C(K,\chi_{\varphi},\chi_{\sigma})\big(\left\lvert{{\partial\Omega}}\right\rvert+\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}\big).\end{split}

(4.33)

On noting Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities and
, we deal with the source terms in (
4.32
) as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{n}\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}+\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)-\mu_{h}^{n}\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\mu_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|{\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|{\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}\left\lvert{\Omega}\right\rvert\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\mu_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}+R_{0}^{2}\left\|{(1+\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert+\left\lvert{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert)}\right\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}\left\lvert{\Omega}\right\rvert\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\mu_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}+\left(3R_{0}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\right)\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\left(3R_{0}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}\right)\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+C(R_{0},\chi_{\varphi},\Omega).\end{split}

(4.34)

In order to control the source terms, we need to derive an estimate for the chemical potential.
Hence, on noting (
3.3
) and Young’s inequality, we receive by testing (
4.23b
) with
\varrho_{h}=\mu_{h}^{n}
that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(A\psi_{1}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{n})+A\psi_{2}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})-\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}\Big)\mu_{h}^{n}\Big]+B\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[AR_{3}(2+\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert+\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\big)\left\lvert{\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert+\chi_{\varphi}\left\lvert{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\Big]+B\left\lvert{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert{\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}+2A^{2}R_{3}^{2}\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}+2A^{2}R_{3}^{2}\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert^{2}+2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}\left\lvert{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\frac{B^{2}}{m_{0}}\left\lvert{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{m_{0}}{4}\left\lvert{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(A,R_{3},\Omega),

which yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}&\leq 4A^{2}R_{3}^{2}\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}\Big)+4\chi_{\varphi}^{2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{2B^{2}}{m_{0}}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{m_{0}}{2}\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(A,R_{3},\Omega).\end{split}

(4.35)

Furthermore, we calculate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\chi_{\varphi}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\big(\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\big)-\chi_{\varphi}\big(\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\big)\sigma_{h}^{n}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})-\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n-1})-\chi_{\varphi}(\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1})(\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1})\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x,\end{split}

(4.36a)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\chi_{\varphi}(\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1})(\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1})\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert\leq\frac{\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.36b)

Moreover, applying the triangle inequality and Young’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\chi_{\sigma}^{2}\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}&\leq 2\left\|{\chi_{\sigma}\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.37)

For the reader’s convenience, we define the constants

\displaystyle\begin{split}&c_{1}\coloneqq\frac{m_{0}}{2},\quad c_{2}\coloneqq\frac{n_{0}\chi_{\sigma}^{2}}{2},\quad c_{3}\coloneqq\frac{K\chi_{\sigma}}{4},\quad c_{4}\coloneqq 2\eta_{0},\quad c_{5}\coloneqq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2\tau_{1}},\quad c_{6}\coloneqq 3R_{0}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}+4\chi_{\varphi}^{2},\quad c_{7}\coloneqq 4A^{2}R_{3}^{2},\\
&c_{8}\coloneqq c_{7}+3R_{0}^{2}+\frac{3\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2}+KC_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}\left(\frac{\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2\chi_{\sigma}}+1\right),\quad c_{9}\coloneqq\frac{2B^{2}}{m_{0}}+KC_{\mathrm{tr}}^{2}\left(\frac{\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{2\chi_{\sigma}}+1\right)+n_{0}\chi_{\varphi}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.38)

Hence, combining (
4.32
)–(
4.37
) and noting (
4.26
) and (
4.38
) gives us

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\Delta t}\mathcal{F}_{h,\delta}(\varphi_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+\frac{B}{2\Delta t}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4\Delta t}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\Delta t}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+c_{1}\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{2}\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+c_{3}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}+c_{4}\|{\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{\alpha\kappa\delta^{2}}{2}\left\|{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{5}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\Delta t}\mathcal{F}_{h,\delta}(\varphi_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{n}^{n-1},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1})+C\big(1+\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}\big)\\
&\quad+c_{6}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\left(\frac{2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}\Delta t}+c_{7}\right)\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\left(\frac{2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}\Delta t}+c_{8}\right)\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+c_{9}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.39)

At this point, if the discrete energy is non-negative, a common strategy would be to absorb the terms from the right-hand side of (
4.39
) with index
n
to the left-hand side, supposed that the time step size
\Delta t
is small enough. This would lead to a discrete energy inequality.
However, if
\chi_{\varphi}\not=0
, the discrete energy can be negative.
Therefore, we continue to bound the product
\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})
with Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities and hence absorb them with the help of the term
A\psi(\varphi_{h}^{n})+\tfrac{\chi_{\sigma}}{2}\left\lvert{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}
from the discrete energy. This is where we make use of
, in particular (
3.2
) and (
3.4
). By Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}(1-\varphi_{h}^{n})\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{2\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+C(\chi_{\sigma},\chi_{\varphi},\Omega).

(4.40)

Moreover, we obtain from (
3.2
) that

\displaystyle R_{1}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}\leq\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\psi(\varphi_{h}^{n})\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x+R_{2}\left\lvert{\Omega}\right\rvert.

(4.41)

Multiplying both sides of (
4.39
) with
\Delta t
, summing from
n=1,...,m
for
m\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
, noting (
4.40
)–(
4.41
) and absorbing the terms on the right-hand side with index
m=n
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big(AR_{1}-\frac{4\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}-c_{8}\Delta t\Big)\|{\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{h}^{2}+\Big(\frac{B}{2}-c_{9}\Delta t\Big)\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Big(\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4}-c_{6}\Delta t\Big)\|{\sigma_{h}^{m}}\|_{h}^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\trace(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}))\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{B}{2}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+c_{1}\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{2}\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{3}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}+c_{4}\|{\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{\alpha\kappa\delta^{2}}{2}\left\|{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{5}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg)\\
&\leq\left\lvert{\mathcal{F}_{h,\delta}(\varphi_{h}^{0},\sigma_{h}^{0},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{0},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0})}\right\rvert+C\left(T+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}\right)+\sum_{n=0}^{m-1}\left(\frac{4\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}+(c_{7}+c_{8})\Delta t\right)\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{m-1}\Big(c_{6}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+c_{9}\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(4.42)

The coefficients on the left-hand side are positive supposed that the time step size
\Delta t
fulfills (
4.43
).

\displaystyle\Delta t

\displaystyle<\Delta t_{*}\coloneqq\min\left\{\frac{B}{2c_{9}},\ \frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{4c_{6}},\ \frac{AR_{1}-\frac{4\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}}{c_{8}}\right\},

(4.43)

where the constants
c_{6},c_{8},c_{9}>0
are defined by (
4.38
) and
AR_{1}-\frac{4\chi_{\varphi}^{2}}{\chi_{\sigma}}>0
due to (
3.4
).
Next, we obtain from a discrete Gronwall argument (i.e. Lemma
4.6
), that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|{\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{m}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\trace(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}))\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{m}\bigg(\alpha\delta^{2}\left\|{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg)\\
&\leq C\Big(T+\left\lvert{\mathcal{F}_{h,\delta}(\varphi_{h}^{0},\sigma_{h}^{0},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{0},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0})}\right\rvert+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{h,{\partial\Omega}}^{2}\Big)\exp(CT)\end{split}

(4.44)

for some constants
C>0
that are independent of
h,\Delta t,\alpha,\delta
.
Noting Korn’s inequality, (
3.29b
), (
3.29g
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
) and taking the maximum over
m=1,...,N_{T}
on the left-hand side of (
4.44
) yield

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max_{n=1,...,N_{T}}\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{[\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}]_{-}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{1}}\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}({\partial\Omega})}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\bigg(\alpha\delta^{2}\left\|{\nabla\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[G^{\prime}_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})+[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\bigg)\\
&\leq C(T)\Big(1+\left\lvert{\mathcal{F}_{h,\delta}(\varphi_{h}^{0},\sigma_{h}^{0},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{0},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0})}\right\rvert+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\Big),\end{split}

(4.45)

for a constant
C(T)>0
that is independent of
h,\Delta t,\alpha,\delta
but depends exponentially on
T
.
On noting (
4.14
), the right-hand side of (
4.45
) is bounded uniformly in
h,\Delta t,\alpha,\delta
. This proves the result.
∎

4.4
Existence of regularized discrete solutions

In the next theorem, we apply a strategy based on Brouwer’s fixed point theorem
[
28
, Chap. 8.1.4, Thm. 3]
in order to prove existence of discrete solutions to
.
Here, one of the main difficulties is to construct specific mappings on a finite dimensional Hilbert space such that Brouwer’s fixed point theorem can be applied in the right way.
It turns out that the testing procedure of Lemma
4.7

is very helpful. However, we need to deal with similar difficulties as in Lemma
4.7
, which explains the minor constraint on the time step size.

Theorem 4.8

(Existence)
.

Let
–
hold true and let
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and assume that
\Delta t<\Delta t_{*}
, where
\Delta t_{*}
is defined in (
4.43
).
Then, for all
n\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
, there exists at least one solution
(\varphi_{h}^{n},\mu_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n},p_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
to the problem
which is stable in the sense of (
4.29
).

We prove existence of solutions to the discrete problem
with the combination of the stability result (
4.29
) and a fixed point argument.
However, we can not directly show existence for solutions
(\varphi_{h}^{n}
,
\mu_{h}^{n}
,
\sigma_{h}^{n}
,
p_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}
,
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
as we have no control over the pressure
p_{h}^{n}
.
Therefore, we first prove existence of functions
(\varphi_{h}^{n}
,
\mu_{h}^{n}
,
\sigma_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}
,
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
which solve
with (
4.23
) replaced by (
4.5
).
Afterwards, we reconstruct the discrete pressure
p_{h}^{n}
and show that
\varphi_{h}^{n}
,
\mu_{h}^{n}
,
\sigma_{h}^{n}
,
p_{h}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}
,
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}
solve the problem
.

First, we define the following inner product on the Hilbert space
(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}

\displaystyle\left({(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\varphi_{h}\zeta_{h}+\mu_{h}\varrho_{h}+\sigma_{h}\xi_{h}+\mathbb{B}_{h}:\mathbb{C}_{h}\Big]+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

for all
(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
.

For some given
(\varphi_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{2}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
, let the mapping

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{h}:(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}

be such that for any
(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\varphi_{h}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}-\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}\Big)\zeta_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[m(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\nabla\mu_{h}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(-\mu_{h}+A\psi_{1}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h})+A\psi_{2}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})-\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}\Big)\varrho_{h}\Big]+B\nabla\varphi_{h}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[K\big(\sigma_{h}-\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}\big)\xi_{h}\Big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}+\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{n}\Big)\xi_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[n(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h})\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\cdot\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\quad\quad

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}2\mathcal{I}_{h}[\eta(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}):\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h})+\kappa\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h})-\mathbb{I}\big]:\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}+\big(\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h})\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}+\frac{\kappa}{\tau(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})}(\mathbb{B}_{h}-\mathbb{I})\Big):\mathbb{C}_{h}\Big]-2\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{C}_{h}\beta_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\quad\quad

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\alpha\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}:\nabla\mathbb{C}_{h}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{d}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}]_{i}\Lambda_{\delta,i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

for all
(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
.
A wanted solution to our problem, if it exists, corresponds to a zero of
\mathcal{H}^{h}
. On noting the definition of
\Lambda_{\delta,i,j}
, it follows that the mapping
\mathcal{H}^{h}
is continuous.

Let
R>0
be given. Let us assume that the continuous mapping
\mathcal{H}^{h}\circ f^{-1}
has no zero which lies in the ball

\displaystyle B_{R}^{h}\coloneqq\{(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}\ :\ |||(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})|||\leq R\},

where

\displaystyle|||(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})|||^{2}\coloneqq\left({(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h}),(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})}\right),

and where the linear transformation
f:(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
and its inverse are given by

\displaystyle f:(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big(\mu_{h},\ \frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\ \chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\ \mathbb{B}_{h}\Big),

\displaystyle f^{-1}:(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big(\Delta t(\varrho_{h}+2\zeta_{h}),\ \zeta_{h},\ \frac{\chi_{\varphi}}{\chi_{\sigma}}\Delta t(\varrho_{h}+2\zeta_{h})+\frac{1}{\chi_{\sigma}}\xi_{h},\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\ \mathbb{C}_{h}\Big).

Then for such
R>0
, we define a continuous mapping
\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}:B_{R}^{h}\to\partial B_{R}^{h}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\coloneqq-R\frac{(\mathcal{H}^{h}\circ f^{-1})(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})}{|||(\mathcal{H}^{h}\circ f^{-1})(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})|||}\quad\quad\forall\ (\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\in B_{R}^{h},

We deduce from Brouwer’s fixed point theorem
[
28
, Chap. 8.1.4, Thm. 3]
that there exists at least one fixed point
(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})=f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})\in B_{R}^{h}
of the mapping
\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}
satisfying

\displaystyle|||f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||=|||(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})|||=|||\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})|||=R.

(4.46)

On noting (
4.8
), (
4.9
), (
4.6
) and (
4.46
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\right\rvert^{2}\big]}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq Ch^{-d}\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\right\rvert^{2}\big]}\|_{L^{1}}=Ch^{-d}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}\leq Ch^{-d}R^{2},

which, on noting (
3.29g
), leads to

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\geq Ch^{\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{1}}\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\geq Ch^{\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}.

(4.47)

Hence, analogously to the proof of (
4.29
), we get together with (
4.47
) that

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq C\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta t}-\frac{1}{\Delta t_{*}}\Big)\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}-G_{\delta}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big(\|{\mu_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)-C(\varphi_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n})

\displaystyle\geq C\min\Big\{1,\ h^{\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta t}-\frac{1}{\Delta t_{*}}\Big)\Big\}|||(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||^{2}-C(\varphi_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}),

where
C(\varphi_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{h}^{n-1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1},\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n})
denotes a constant that depends on
\varphi_{h}^{n-1}
,
\sigma_{h}^{n-1}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}
,
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}
,
\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}
but not on
\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}
.
We remark that both
|||\cdot|||
and
|||f(\cdot)|||
define norms on
(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{3}\times\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
. Hence, due to norm equivalence in finite dimensions, there exist constants
c_{1}(h),c_{2}(h)>0
(which can depend on
h
in general) such that

\displaystyle c_{1}(h)|||(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||\leq|||f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||\leq c_{2}(h)|||(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||.

Hence, with (
4.46
) and with
R>0
large enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)>0.

On the other side, as
(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})=f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})\in B_{R}^{h}
is a fixed point of
\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}
, it holds

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{|||\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||}{R}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left({(\mathcal{G}_{R}^{h}\circ f)(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{|||\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||}{R}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left({f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{|||\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||}{R}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\Big(\|{\mu_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}+\left\|{\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h}}\right\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}}\|_{h}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{B}_{h}:(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h}))]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)

\displaystyle\leq-C\frac{|||\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||}{R}\Big(\min\big\{1,h^{\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}\big\}|||f(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})|||^{2}-1\Big),

where, in the last step, we used (
3.29h
) and argued like in (
4.47
).
Therefore, with (
4.46
) and with
R>0
large enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{H}^{h}(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h}),\Big(\mu_{h},\frac{\varphi_{h}}{\Delta t}-2\mu_{h},\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}-\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\frac{\kappa}{2}\big(\mathbb{I}-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big)}\right)<0,

which yields a contradiction. Hence, if
R>0
is large enough, the mapping
\mathcal{H}^{h}\circ f^{-1}
possesses a zero
(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})
in
B_{R}^{h}
. Moreover,
(\varphi_{h},\mu_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})=f^{-1}(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})
corresponds to a solution of our problem.

To obtain the existence of a discrete pressure
p_{h}^{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{h}
and therefore to justify (
4.23
), we proceed as follows. Equation (
4.23
) defines a linear functional
\mathcal{V}_{h}\to\mathbb{R}
which vanishes on
\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}
. The existence of a unique pressure
p_{h}^{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\cap L^{2}_{0}(\Omega)
follows directly from, e.g.,
[
34
, Chap. I, Lem 4.1]
or
[
13
, Lem. 4.2]
on noting the discrete LBB stability condition (
4.2
) of the discrete velocity–pressure spaces.

The stability result (
4.29
) follows from Lemma
4.7
. This proves the theorem.
∎

4.5
Existence of unregularized discrete solutions

Now let us consider a finite element approximation of
without the regularization parameter
\delta
and with a positive definite discrete Cauchy–Green tensor.

Problem
:

For given discrete initial and boundary data satisfying (
4.14
) and
n\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
, find the discrete solution
(\varphi_{h}^{n},\mu_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n},p_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}
which satisfies, for any
(\zeta_{h},\varrho_{h},\xi_{h},q_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h},\mathbb{C}_{h})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h}
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}-\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}\Big)\zeta_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[m(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

(4.48a)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(-\mu_{h}^{n}+A\psi_{1}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{n})+A\psi_{2}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})-\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{n}\Big)\varrho_{h}\Big]+B\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

(4.48b)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}+\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{n}\Big)\xi_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[n(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[K(\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n})\xi_{h}\big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1},

(4.48c)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right)q_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

(4.48d)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\right)+2\mathcal{I}_{h}[\eta(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}):\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h})\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}\kappa(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{I}):\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}-\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}}\right)p_{h}^{n}+\big(\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

(4.48e)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}+\frac{\kappa}{\tau(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{I})\Big):\mathbb{C}_{h}\Big]-2\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}^{n}_{h}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}\big]+\alpha\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}:\nabla\mathbb{C}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{d}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}]_{i}\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.48f)

Here, the nonlinear function
\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{C}_{h})
for
\mathbb{C}_{h}\in\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}
is defined similarly to
\Lambda_{\delta,i,j}(\tilde{}\mathbb{C}_{h})
for
\tilde{}\mathbb{C}_{h}\in\mathcal{W}_{h}
, see
[
6
, Rem. 5.1]
.
Moreover, the analogues of (
4.21
),(
4.22
) without
\delta
-regularization follow with the same arguments. In particular, for
k\in\{1,...,N_{k}\}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{B}_{h}^{-1}\big]

\displaystyle=-\partial_{x_{i}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\trace(\ln\mathbb{B}_{h})\big]\qquad

\displaystyle\text{on }K_{k},

(4.49)

\displaystyle\|{\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h})}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\qquad

\displaystyle\forall\ \mathbb{B}_{h}\in\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}.

(4.50)

Here we note that
H_{\delta}(G_{\delta}^{\prime}(s))\to\ln(s^{-1})=-\ln(s)
and
\beta_{\delta}(s)\to s
for all
s>0
, as
\delta\to 0
.

Now we define the unregularized energy
\mathcal{F}_{h}:\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}\to\mathbb{R}
of the problem
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{F}_{h}(\varphi_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})&=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[A\psi(\varphi_{h})+\frac{\chi_{\sigma}}{2}\left\lvert{\sigma_{h}}\right\rvert^{2}+\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}(1-\varphi_{h})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}-\ln(\mathbb{B}_{h})\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}\frac{B}{2}\left\lvert{\nabla\varphi_{h}}\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\right\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,\end{split}

(4.51)

for all
(\varphi_{h},\sigma_{h},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h},\mathbb{B}_{h})\in\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{S}_{h}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}
.

Next, we obtain existence and stability of solutions to the problem
by passing to the limit
\delta\to 0
in the regularized discrete problem
and in the
a priori
bounds (
4.29
).
This can be achieved analogously to
[
6
, Thm. 5.2]
.
We remark that the positive definiteness of the discrete Cauchy–Green tensor for the problem
is guaranteed as we can control the negative eigenvalues and the inverse of the discrete Cauchy–Green tensor from the
\delta
-regularized problem
, which is due to (
4.29
).
Moreover, as we have no control over the pressure of the regularized problem
, the existence of a pressure for the problem
can still be established with the discrete LBB stability condition (
4.2
).

Theorem 4.9

(Solutions to the unregularized discrete problem)
.

Let
–
hold. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and assume that
\Delta t<\Delta t_{*}
, where
\Delta t_{*}
is defined in (
4.43
).
Then, for all
n\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
, there exists at least one solution
(\varphi_{h}^{n},\mu_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n},p_{h}^{n},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})\in(\mathcal{S}_{h})^{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{h}\times\mathcal{W}_{h,\mathrm{PD}}
to the unregularized discrete problem
with
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}
being positive definite.
Moreover, all solutions of
are stable in the sense that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max_{n=1,...,N_{T}}\Big(\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert\big]}\right\|_{L^{1}}\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}-\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(\|{\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}({\partial\Omega})}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\trace\big(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}+[\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}]^{-1}-2\mathbb{I}\big)\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\\
&\leq C(T)\Big(1+\left\lvert{\mathcal{F}_{h}(\varphi_{h}^{0},\sigma_{h}^{0},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{0},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0})}\right\rvert+\Delta t\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\Big)\leq C(T),\end{split}

(4.52)

where the constants
C(T)>0
are independent of
h,\Delta t,\alpha
but depend exponentially on
T
.

4.6
Improving the regularity results in arbitrary dimensions

In the following, we derive higher order estimates for discrete solution of
in arbitrary dimensions
d\in\{2,3\}
.
For the next steps, we require the
L^{2}
projectors
\mathcal{P}_{h}:\mathbf{V}\to\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{h}:H^{1}(\Omega)\to\mathcal{S}_{h}
defined by

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{P}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\quad\quad

\displaystyle\forall\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\in\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}},

(4.53)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{h}\varrho\zeta_{h}\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\varrho\zeta_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\quad\quad

\displaystyle\forall\ \zeta_{h}\in\mathcal{S}_{h},

(4.54)

which fulfill, as
\Omega
is convex and the family
\{\mathcal{T}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is quasi-uniform, that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}\|_{H^{1}}\quad\quad\forall\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathbf{V},

(4.55)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Q}_{h}\zeta}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\zeta}\|_{H^{1}}\quad\quad\forall\ \zeta\in H^{1}(\Omega),

(4.56)

see, e.g.,
[
6
]
and references therein. Analogously to (
4.54
), we also introduce a matrix valued projection operator
\mathcal{Q}_{h}:H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\mathrm{S}})\to\mathcal{W}_{h}
which fulfills a stability estimate corresponding to (
4.56
), see
[
6
]
.

Now, we improve the regularity for the order parameter and the nutrient.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
–
hold. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and let
\Delta t<\Delta t_{*}
, where
\Delta t_{*}
is defined in (
4.43
). Then, in addition to (
4.52
), all solutions of
fulfill for any
l\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
,

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left(\|{\Delta_{h}\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\frac{\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{2}+\left\|{\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{4/d}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(T),

(4.57a)

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n+l}-\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)l\Delta t,

(4.57b)

where the constants
C(T)
are independent of
\alpha,h,\Delta t
, but depend exponentially on
T
.

The first estimate in (
4.57a
) can easily be shown by choosing
\varrho_{h}=\Delta_{h}\varphi_{h}^{n}
in (
4.48b
) and using (
4.15
),
, (
4.14
), (
4.52
), (
4.9
) together with Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities.

For the reader’s convenience, we show the third estimate in (
4.57a
) and we note that the second estimate in (
4.57a
) follows with similar arguments.
Let
\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega)
. Then, on choosing
\xi_{h}=\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{h}
in (
4.48
) and noting (
4.54
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big)\xi\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big)\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi-\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Gamma_{\sigma}(\varphi_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n})\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[n(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})\big]\big(\chi_{\sigma}\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}\big)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\partial\Omega}K\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[(\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n})\mathcal{Q}_{h}\xi\Big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}.

Hence, on noting (
4.56
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
),
, Hölder’s inequality and the trace theorem, we get

\displaystyle\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big)\xi\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\Big)\|{\xi}\|_{H^{1}}.

By Hölder’s inequality, a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality and the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{q}(\Omega)
with
1\leq q\leq 6
for
d\in\{2,3\}
, we receive

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}\begin{cases}\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2},&\text{ if }d=2,\\
\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{3}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{6}}\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}},&\text{ if }d=3.\end{cases}

This leads to

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|{\frac{\sigma_{h}^{n}-\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{\frac{4}{d}}\leq C\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(1+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\sigma_{\infty,h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\Big)^{\frac{4}{d}},

where the right-hand side is bounded due to (
4.52
), (
4.14
) and Hölder’s inequality. This shows the third estimate in (
4.57a
).

Next, we set
\zeta_{h}=\Delta t(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})
in (
4.48a
), where
m\in\{0,...,N_{T}-l\}
and
l\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
, to obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\varphi_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}-\Delta t\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}\Big)(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})\Big]+\Delta t\Big(\mathcal{I}_{h}[m(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Summing from
n=m+1,...,m+l
gives

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\left\lvert{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\right\rvert^{2}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\Delta t\sum_{n=m+1}^{m+l}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{n}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta t\sum_{n=m+1}^{m+l}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[m(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})\big]\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})-\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m})\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

which yields on noting (
4.9
),
,
, Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{q}(\Omega)
with
1\leq q\leq 6
for
d\in\{2,3\}
, (
4.52
) and (
4.14
), that

\displaystyle\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Delta t\sum_{n=m+1}^{m+l}\Big(\|{\Gamma_{\varphi}(\varphi_{h}^{n},\sigma_{h}^{n},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{6}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{3}}\Big)\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta t\sum_{n=m+1}^{m+l}\Big(1+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{l}\Big(1+\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{H^{1}}.

Multiplying both sides by
\Delta t
, summing from
m=0,...,N_{T}-l
, applying Hölder’s inequality and noting (
4.52
) and (
4.14
) leads to

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{m=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C(T)(\Delta t)^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{l}\sum_{m=0}^{N_{T}-l}\Big(1+\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Delta t\sum_{k=1}^{l}\left(1+\left(\Delta t\sum_{m=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\nabla\mu_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\Delta t\sum_{m=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m+k}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(\Delta t\sum_{m=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\varphi_{h}^{m+l}-\varphi_{h}^{m}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)l\Delta t.

This proves the lemma.

∎

4.7
Improving the regularity results in two dimensions

The next result contains ideas of
[
6
, Thm. 7.1]
.
We provide a regularity result for the left Cauchy–Green tensor in two space dimensions, supposed that a CFL condition for the time step size is fulfilled.
The restriction to two space dimensions is due to a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality for
d=2
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
–
hold true. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and assume

\displaystyle\Delta t\leq\min\{\Delta t_{*},\ c_{*}(T)\alpha^{2}h^{2}\},

(4.58)

where
\Delta t_{*}
is defined in (
4.43
) and
c_{*}(T)>0
is a (probably very small) constant which is independent of
\alpha,h,\Delta t
but can depend on
T
. Then, in addition to (
4.52
), (
4.57a
), (
4.57b
), the following bound holds for all solutions of
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\limits_{n=1,...,N_{T}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left(\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\|{\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\left\|{\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{4/3}\right)\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1}),\end{split}

(4.59)

where the constant
C(T,\alpha^{-1})>0
is independent of
h,\Delta t
, but depends exponentially on
T,\alpha^{-1}
.

On choosing
\mathbb{C}_{h}=\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}
in (
4.48
), it follows from (
4.27
),
, Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\Delta t\frac{\kappa}{2\tau_{1}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+\Delta t\alpha\|{\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{h}^{2}+\Delta t\frac{\kappa}{4\tau_{1}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{h}^{2}+C\Delta t\Big(1+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}\big]}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}\max\limits_{i,j=1,2}\|{\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{4}}\|{\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\Big).\end{split}

(4.60)

A calculation from
[
6
, Thm. 7.1]
and a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality for
d=2
yield

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\left\lvert{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert^{2}\big]}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\max\limits_{i,j=1,2}\|{\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\leq C\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\leq C\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

(4.61)

It follows from a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality for
d=2
, (
4.52
), (
4.14c
) and the Poincaré inequality that

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\leq C(T)\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}.

(4.62)

Combining (
4.60
)–(
4.62
) gives together with (
4.9
), (
4.52
) and a (generalized) Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\frac{\kappa}{2\tau_{1}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\Delta t\alpha\|{\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(T)\Delta t\Big(1+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\min\left\{\frac{\kappa}{4\tau_{1}},\alpha\right\}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+C(T)\Delta t+C(T)\alpha^{-2}\Delta t\Big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Summing from
n=1,...,m
, where
m\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
and absorbing the terms with index
n=m
to the left-hand side yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big(1-C(T)\alpha^{-2}\Delta t\big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\Big)\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\min\left\{\frac{\kappa}{4\tau_{1}},\alpha\right\}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big)\\
&\leq C(T)\Big(\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\Big)+C(T)\alpha^{-2}\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{m-1}\Big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.63)

On noting (
4.6
), (
4.52
) and (
4.14c
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq Ch^{-2}\big(\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{m-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\leq C(T)h^{-2}.

Hence, if
\Delta t\leq\min\{\Delta t_{*},c_{*}(T)\alpha^{2}h^{2}\}
for a (probably very small) constant
c_{*}(T)>0
which depends on
T
but not on
h,\Delta t,\alpha
, then the coefficient of
\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
on the left-hand side of (
4.63
) is positive. Then, we deduce from a discrete Gronwall argument (i.e. Lemma
4.6
) that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{m}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum\limits_{n=1}^{m}\Big(\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Delta t\min\left\{\frac{\kappa}{4\tau},\alpha\right\}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\Big)\exp\Big(C(T)\alpha^{-2}\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\Big).

Applying (
4.14c
), (
4.14d
) and (
4.52
) proves the first three bounds in (
4.59
).

Let
\mathbb{C}_{h}\in\mathcal{W}_{h}
. Then, a straightforward calculation yields on noting
Hölder’s inequality, a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality and (
4.59
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}^{n}_{h}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert\leq C\|{\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{L^{4}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{4}}\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\|{\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}\\
&\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})\|{\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}.\end{split}

(4.64)

Further, it holds with Hölder’s inequality, (
4.61
), (
4.52
), (
4.14c
), (
4.59
) and a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}]_{i}\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert&\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}\max_{i,j=1,2}\|{\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n})}\|_{L^{4}}\|{\nabla\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}\\
&\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{C}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}.\end{split}

(4.65)

Let
\mathbb{C}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\text{S}})
. On choosing
\mathbb{C}_{h}=\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}\in\mathcal{W}_{h}
in (
4.48
), we obtain on noting (
4.54
) and Hölder’s inequality, that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big):\mathbb{C}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big):\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\left[\frac{\kappa}{\tau(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}):\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}^{n}_{h}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}\Big]-\alpha\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}:\nabla\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{d}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}]_{i}\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathcal{Q}_{h}\mathbb{C}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

which, on noting (
4.56
), (
4.64
) and (
4.65
), yields

\displaystyle\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\Big):\mathbb{C}\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})\Big(1+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\Big)\|{\mathbb{C}}\|_{H^{1}}.

This yields

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|{\frac{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{4/3}

\displaystyle\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(1+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{4/3}+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2/3}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{4/3}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2/3}\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}^{2/3}\Big)

On noting (
4.52
), (
4.14c
), the first and the third bounds in (
4.59
) and a Hölder inequality, we obtain the last bound in (
4.59
).
∎

Now we have more control over the left Cauchy–Green tensor. This makes it possible to prove a regularity result for the discrete time derivative for the velocity.

First, we introduce the Helmholtz–Stokes operator
\mathcal{S}:\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{V}
such that
\mathcal{S}\boldsymbol{u}
is the unique solution to the Helmholtz–Stokes problem

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\mathcal{S}\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}+\nabla(\mathcal{S}\boldsymbol{u}):\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\left<{\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}}\right>_{\mathbf{V}}\quad\quad\forall\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\in\mathbf{V},

(4.66)

where
\left<{\cdot,\cdot}\right>_{\mathbf{V}}
denotes the duality pairing between
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{V}
. We remark that
\|{\mathcal{S}\cdot}\|_{H^{1}}
and
\|{\cdot}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}
are equivalent norms on
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}
, see, e.g.,
[
11
]
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
–
hold. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and that the CFL constraint (
4.58
) holds. Then, in addition to (
4.52
), (
4.57a
), (
4.57b
), (
4.59
), all solutions of
fulfill

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|{\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1}),

(4.67)

where the constant
C(T,\alpha^{-1})>0
is depending exponentially on
T,\alpha^{-1}
, but is independent of
h,\Delta t
.

On choosing
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}=\mathcal{P}_{h}\Big[\mathcal{S}\big(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\big)\Big]\in\mathcal{V}_{h,\text{div}}\subset\mathcal{V}_{h}
in (
4.48
), we obtain on noting (
4.66
), (
4.53
), (
4.55
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\right)\cdot\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}\cdot\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\right)\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\Omega}2\mathcal{I}_{h}[\eta(\varphi_{h}^{n-1})]\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}):\mathbb{D}\left(\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]\right)+\kappa\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}-\mathbb{I}\big]:\nabla\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\Omega}\big(\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})\big)\cdot\mathcal{P}_{h}\left[\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\Big(1+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert}\right\|_{L^{4/3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})}\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{2}\Big).

With Hölder’s inequality, a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality for
d=2
, (
4.52
), (
4.14c
), the Poincaré inequality and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big),

and, with similar arguments,

\displaystyle\left\|{\left\lvert{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right\rvert}\right\|_{L^{4/3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{3}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{3}\Big),

which leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}&\leq C(T)\Big(1+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{3}\\
&\quad+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{3}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})}\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(4.68)

Next, we need an estimate for the last term in (
4.68
).
Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
On noting Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev embedding along with a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality, (
4.52
) and (
4.14b
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert{\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{4}}\big(\|{\nabla\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\nabla\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}}\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\big(\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}\big)\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\big(\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+1\big)\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}}\|_{H^{1}}.

This yields

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})}\right\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{4/3}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\big(\|{\sigma_{h}^{n}}\|_{H^{1}}+1\big)^{2/3}\|{\sigma_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{H^{1}}^{4/3},

where the right-hand side is bounded due to a Hölder inequality, (
4.52
) and (
4.14b
).
From similar arguments, we deduce

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}}\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{2}\leq C(T).

Taking the
\frac{2}{3}
power on both sides of (
4.68
), multiplying by
\Delta t
and summing from
n=1,...,N_{T}
, we then get

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|{\mathcal{S}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}\right)}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{4/3}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Delta t\sum\limits_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\Big(1+\|{\mathbb{B}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\varphi_{h}^{n-1}\nabla\mu_{h}^{n}+\sigma_{h}^{n-1}\nabla(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{n}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{n})}\|_{(H^{1})^{\prime}}^{4/3}\Big).

This leads to (
4.67
), on noting (
4.52
), (
4.59
), (
4.14
), Hölder’s inequality and the calculations from above.
∎

At this point, we note that the bound (
4.67
) is not useful to apply common compactness techniques based on Aubin–Lions.
The problem is that the discrete velocity belongs to
\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}}\subset H^{1}_{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, but
\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}}
is no subspace of
\mathbf{V}
, as the discrete velocity is only divergence-free with respect to the ansatz space
\mathcal{S}_{h}
.
Moreover,
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
is compactly embedded in
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, but there exists no injective mapping from
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}
. This is why Aubin–Lions cannot be applied here.

However, there are other techniques one can use for the velocity, see, e.g.,
[
6
,
7
,
36
]
.
In this work, we follow the strategy of Metzger
[
49
]
which is based on
[
5
]
and we introduce the orthogonal Stokes projector
\mathcal{R}_{h}:\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}}\to\mathbf{V}
by

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}:\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}:\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\quad\quad\forall\ \boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\in\mathbf{V}.

(4.69)

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}\in\mathcal{V}_{h,\mathrm{div}}
, it holds (cf.
[
36
]
)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{H^{1}},

(4.70a)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch\|{\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\right)}\|_{L^{2}},

(4.70b)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(h\|{\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\right)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}\big).

(4.70c)

The following result is based on the bounds (
4.70c
), (
4.52
) and (
4.67
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
–
hold. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
) and that the CFL constraint (
4.58
) holds. Then, there exists a constant
C(T,\alpha^{-1})>0
depending exponentially on
T,\alpha^{-1}
but not on
h,\Delta t
, such that, in addition to (
4.52
), (
4.57a
), (
4.57b
), (
4.59
), (
4.67
), all solutions of
satisfy for all
l\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}
,

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{2}\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})\big(l^{\frac{3}{4}}\Delta t+h^{2}\big).

(4.71)

It follows from (
4.70c
) that

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\mathcal{R}_{h}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{2}\leq C\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\Big(\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{2}+h^{2}\|{\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\right)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)\eqqcolon I+II.

On applying a Hölder’s inequality, we obtain for the first term

\displaystyle I\leq C\bigg(\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\bigg)^{\frac{3}{4}}\bigg(\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}},

where the second term on the right-hand side is bounded due to (
4.52
). By a triangle inequality and (
4.67
), we get

\displaystyle\Delta t\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}\|{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+l}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n}}\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta t\sum_{m=1}^{l}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{T}-l}(\Delta t)^{\frac{4}{3}}\left\|{\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+m}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{n+m-1}}{\Delta t}}\right\|_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime}}^{\frac{4}{3}}\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})l(\Delta t)^{\frac{4}{3}},

which yields
I\leq C(T,\alpha^{-1})l^{\frac{3}{4}}\Delta t
.
Moreover, it holds by (
4.52
) that
II\leq C(T)h^{2}
.
This shows the result.
∎

4.8
Passage to the limit and convergence to a weak solution

In the following, we prove that there exists a weak solution of
in the sense of Definition
3.2
which is obtained from converging subsequences of discrete solutions of
by passing to the limit
(h,\Delta t)\to(0,0)
.

For future reference, we recall the following compactness results from
[
53
, Sect. 8, Cor. 4 and Thm. 5]
.
Let
X,Y,Z
be Banach spaces with a compact embedding
X\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow Y
and a continuous embedding
Y\hookrightarrow Z
. Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
r>1
. Then, the following embeddings are compact:

\displaystyle\{\eta\in L^{p}(0,T;X)

\displaystyle\mid\partial_{t}\eta\in L^{1}(0,T;Z)\}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{p}(0,T;Y),

(4.72a)

\displaystyle\{\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T;X)

\displaystyle\mid\partial_{t}\eta\in L^{r}(0,T;Z)\}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow C([0,T];Y).

(4.72b)

Moreover, let
F
be a bounded subset in
L^{p}(0,T;X)
with

\displaystyle\lim_{\theta\to 0}\|{\eta(\cdot+\theta)-\eta(\cdot)}\|_{L^{p}(0,T;Z)}=0\quad\text{ uniformly for }\eta\in F.

(4.72c)

Then
F
is relatively compact in
L^{p}(0,T;Y)
if
1\leq p<\infty
and in
C([0,T];Y)
if
p=\infty
, respectively.

Furthermore, we recall the following “compactness by perturbation” result from Azérad and Guillén-González
[
5
]
which provides strong convergence for subsequences of the discrete velocity.
Let
X,Y,Z
be like before. Let
\{f_{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon>0}
be a family of functions which is bounded in
L^{p}(0,T;X)
with
1\leq p<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\|{f_{\epsilon}(\cdot+\theta)-f_{\epsilon}(\cdot)}\|_{L^{p}(0,T;Z)}\leq g_{1}(\theta)+g_{2}(\epsilon),

(4.72d)

with
g_{1}(\theta)\to 0
as
\theta\to 0
and
g_{2}(\epsilon)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
. Then, the family
\{f_{\epsilon}\}_{\epsilon>0}
possesses a cluster point in
L^{p}(0,T;Y)
as
\epsilon\to 0
.

Let us introduce the following notation for affine linear and piecewise constant extensions of time discrete function
a^{n}(\cdot)
,
n=0,...,N_{T}
:

\displaystyle a^{\Delta t}(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{t-t^{n-1}}{\Delta t}a^{n}(\cdot)+\frac{t^{n}-t}{\Delta t}a^{n-1}(\cdot)\quad\quad

\displaystyle t\in[t^{n-1},t^{n}],n\in\{1,...,N_{T}\},

(4.73)

\displaystyle a^{\Delta t,+}(\cdot,t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq a^{n}(\cdot),\quad\quad a^{\Delta t,-}(\cdot,t)\coloneqq a^{n-1}(\cdot)\quad\quad

\displaystyle t\in(t^{n-1},t^{n}],n\in\{1,...,N_{T}\}.

(4.74)

Let us note that we write
a^{\Delta t,\pm}
for results that hold true for both
a^{\Delta t,+}
and
a^{\Delta t,-}
, and we write
a^{\Delta t(,\pm)}
for results that hold true for
a^{\Delta t}
,
a^{\Delta t,+}
and
a^{\Delta t,-}
, respectively.

Using this notation, we reformulate the problem
continuously in time. Multiplying each equation by
\Delta t
and summing from
n=1,...,N_{T}
, we obtain for any test functions
(\zeta_{h}
,
\varrho_{h}
,
\xi_{h}
,
q_{h}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}
,
\mathbb{C}_{h})\in(L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{S}_{h}))^{4}\times L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{V}_{h})\times L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{W}_{h})
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\big(\partial_{t}\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t}-\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{\Delta t,+}\big)\zeta_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[m(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-})]\nabla\mu_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}-\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75a)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(-\mu_{h}^{\Delta t,+}+A\psi_{1}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,+})+A\psi_{2}^{\prime}(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-})-\chi_{\varphi}\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\Big)\varrho_{h}\Big]+B\nabla\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\cdot\nabla\varrho_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75b)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\big(\partial_{t}\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t}+\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{\Delta t,+}\big)\xi_{h}\Big]+\mathcal{I}_{h}[n(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-})]\nabla\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,+}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\big)\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}-\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\cdot\nabla\xi_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[K\big(\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,+}-\sigma_{\infty,h}^{\Delta t,+}\big)\xi_{h}\Big]\ \mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75c)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}}\right)q_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75d)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\cdot\left(\left(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\cdot\nabla\right)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega_{T}}2\mathcal{I}_{h}[\eta(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-})]\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}):\mathbb{D}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h})+\kappa(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}-\mathbb{I}):\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}-\operatorname{div}\left({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}}\right)p_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega_{T}}\Big(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\nabla\mu_{h}^{\Delta t,+}+\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,-}\nabla\big(\chi_{\sigma}\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,+}-\chi_{\varphi}\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\big)\Big)\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75e)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{T}}\mathcal{I}_{h}\Big[\Big(\partial_{t}\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t}+\frac{\kappa}{\tau(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,-})}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}-\mathbb{I})\Big):\mathbb{C}_{h}\Big]-2\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}:\mathcal{I}_{h}\big[\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}\big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega_{T}}\alpha\nabla\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}:\nabla\mathbb{C}_{h}-\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{d}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t,-}]_{i}\Lambda_{i,j}(\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+}):\partial_{x_{j}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.75f)

subject to the initial conditions
\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t}(0)=\varphi_{h}^{0}
,
\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t}(0)=\sigma_{h}^{0}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t}(0)=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{0}
and
\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t}(0)=\mathbb{B}_{h}^{0}
, where we write
\Gamma_{\varphi,h}^{\Delta t,+}=\Gamma_{\varphi}(\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t,+},\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t,+},\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t,+})
and similarly for
\Gamma_{\sigma,h}^{\Delta t,+}
.

The following result is a direct consequence of (
4.73
), (
4.74
), Theorem
4.9
, (
4.52
), (
4.57a
), (
4.57b
), (
4.59
), (
4.67
), (
4.71
) and (
4.14
).

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
–
hold. Suppose that the discrete initial and boundary data satisfy (
4.14
). Moreover, assume that
\Delta t<\Delta t_{*}
, where
\Delta t_{*}
is defined in (
4.43
). Then, there exist functions
\varphi_{h}^{\Delta t(,\pm)}
,

\mu_{h}^{\Delta t,+}
,

\sigma_{h}^{\Delta t(,\pm)}
,

p_{h}^{\Delta t,+}
,

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}_{h}^{\Delta t(,\pm)}
,

\mathbb{B}_{h}^{\Delta t(,\pm)}
solving (
4.75a
)–(
4.75
) and constants
C(T)>0
depending on
T
but not on
\alpha,h,\Delta t
, such that
